巧新(1563 TT) Trading Buy

2026、 2027 年營運動能向上，投資建議 Trading Buy。  
投資建議 Trading Buy ：巧新(1563 TT) 努力進行轉型，半導體
新事業於 2H26 逐漸取得進展，且再生鋁出貨占比也明顯拉升，
帶動毛利率明顯改善。2026 年獲利表現回升且 2027 年獲利能
力續揚。惟考量近期汽車零組件族群評價不易拉升，投資建議
Trading Buy，目標價 76 元 (以 2027 年 EPS 及 PE 15 倍評價)。  
3Q26 本業獲利小幅增長，因業外收入挹注，整體獲利顯著季增；
預估 4Q26 獲利相對下降：3Q26 營收 20.16 億元，QoQ+1.83%、
YoY+18.48%。受惠於再生鋁採用比重提升且匯率相對有利，毛
利率由 2Q26 的 24.32%提升至 25.90%。但因運輸成本上漲，營
業費用同步增長，營業利益 QoQ+6.72%、 YoY+211.71%。因業
外認列大筆匯兌利益，稅率表現穩定。稅後純益 2.47 億元，
QoQ+247.56%、 YoY+478.06%， EPS 1.46 元。4Q26 方面，預估
營收 19.21 億元，QoQ-4.70%、 YoY+16.97%。獲利能力方面，
受惠於再生鋁採用率高，預估毛利率提升至 27.54%。因運費相
對下降，預估營業利益 QoQ+15.87%、 YoY+342.49%。因 3Q26
已認列大筆匯兌收益，若以保守角度看待 4Q26 業外貢獻，預估
稅後純益 1.67 億元，QoQ-32.69%、YoY 轉虧為盈，EPS 0.98 元。 
預估 2026 年獲利表現回升，2027 年獲利能力續揚：2026 年方
面，輪圈出貨小幅提升，而半導體相關零組件自 2H26 開始貢獻
初步營收。預估 2026 年營收 77.89 億元，YoY+11.63%。隨著再
生鋁採用率提升且營收規模增長，毛利率有望逐季提升，預估
2026 年毛利率提升至 24.97%，營業利益 YoY+43.13%。若業外
較無匯兌損失影響，預估稅後純益 6.16 億元，YoY+93.56%，
EPS 3.64 元。2027 年方面，根據車廠預示量，鋁圈出貨量將由
2026 年的 60 萬顆提升至 70 萬顆，有助於帶動車用產品營收增
長。而半導體營收占比有機會提升至 5~10%，將提供額外營收
動能。因再生鋁採用比重提升，且半導體產品毛利率相對較高，
2027 年毛利率及獲利能力有望優於 2026 年。預估 2027 年營收
92.77 億元，YoY+19.10%，稅後純益 8.56 億元，YoY+38.99%，
EPS 5.06 元。

2 群益投顧 
  巧新(1563 TT) ESG：  
Environmental 環境保護：(1)鋁下腳料 100%回收，製成再生鋁棒，持續循環
再利用。(2)導入各式節能設備具體節能，已降低溫室氣體排放量。(3)屏東
廠自建太陽能光電。Social 社會責任：(1)獲得 ASI(鋁業管理倡議)績效標準
認證。(2)定期進行人權盡職調查與評估，流程和政策涵蓋重要的人權問題，
保障員工和活動參與者的安全。(3)響應地方政府公益活動。Governance 公
司治理：(1)風險管理依公司程序辦理，內控嚴謹，並設有專職稽核部門定期
查核。(2)將誠信經營納入新人教育訓練課程與人力資源政策中，設立明確
有效之獎懲及申訴機制。(3)每年定期發布 ESG 報告書。

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 12 日

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 上週三（11/18）聯準會公布 10 月 FOMC 會議紀要，幾乎所有官員均認為，若
經濟發展符合預期，仍有進一步升息的必要，部分官員甚至認為，若通膨持續
高於預期，不排除升息 2 碼。此外，聯準會理事沃勒也表示，12 月會議可能升
息 2 碼，或釋出 2027 年加快升息的訊號，顯示聯準會對通膨風險仍保持警
戒，市場升息預期持續升溫。
⚫ 聯準會 10 月會議紀要亦顯示，官員開始關注 AI 投資及資產價格偏高所帶來的
金融穩定風險，尤其 AI 相關債務快速增加，部分融資結構複雜且不透明，且
風險集中於少數企業，可能對金融體系及整體經濟帶來影響。
⚫ 歐洲央行副總裁武伊契奇表示，AI 投資及主權債務已成為目前重要的金融穩定
風險。隨著科技企業融資需求持續增加，相關資金規模及融資結構日益複雜，
可能對債券及股票市場帶來更廣泛影響。
⚫ 上週市場傳出，歐洲央行決策官員計畫討論調高銀行最低存款準備金率，由目

前的 1%提高至 2%，藉此減少歐洲央行利息支出，並自金融體系回收部分資
金；相關議題預計於 11/25 會議討論，實際決策可能延至 12 月。
⚫ 上週三（11/18）日本央行總裁植田和男表示，考量目前金融環境仍偏寬鬆，央
行應持續升息，並依據經濟、物價及金融環境變化調整貨幣政策，以使基礎通
膨逐步穩定在 2%左右，顯示日本央行仍維持貨幣政策正常化方向。
⚫ 上週二（11/17）澳洲央行公布 11 月貨幣政策會議紀要，多數委員認為通膨風
險仍偏向上行，且目前貨幣政策緊縮程度不足以抑制通膨，9 名委員中有 6 名
支持進一步升息，顯示澳洲央行對通膨仍保持警戒。

 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/23/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.830，低點在 33.040，以 32.936 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 9.8 分。 
    上週台幣震盪走貶。週初台幣隨著外資雙向進出而止跌回穩，惟美國科技股賣
壓加重，台股持續走弱，外資賣超台股並轉為匯出，台幣再度走貶。週間美元指數
持續走強，聯準會官員偏鷹派談話及市場對後續升息預期升溫，也使亞幣普遍承壓，
台幣盤中一度貶破 33 元整數關卡，最低來到 33.040；不過，出口商於 33 元上方拋
匯意願積極，加上央行持續進場調節，台幣貶幅逐漸收斂，匯價主要於 32.900-33.000
之間來回震盪，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.936。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣走勢偏弱震盪。就國際市場來看，美國 10 月零售銷售溫和成長，
顯示美國需求端依然強勁，加上聯準會官員偏鷹派談話及後續升息預期持續發酵，
美元指數逼近 104 整數關卡，市場預期美國利率將維持高檔，台美利差仍處相對高
位，不利台幣表現。就台灣市場來看，外資持續調節台股並匯出，近期科技股賣壓
也使台股承壓，資金外流帶給台幣貶值的壓力；不過，出口商於 33 元上方拋匯意願
積極，加上央行持續進場調節，有助於限制台幣貶值速度。綜合以上，預估本週台
幣偏弱震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.850-33.050

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/23/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪。週初境外人民幣主要於 6.6650 附近整理；周間，美元指
數受到美國通膨數據及聯準會官員偏鷹派談話支撐，升至 103.5 上方，人民幣升值
動能受到限制，惟 中國出口結匯需求持續提供支撐，加上人行中間價維持偏強設定，
人民幣在美元走強下仍展現相對韌性。臨近週末，中間價小跌，且美股重挫令市場
避險情緒升溫，境外人民幣走跌，最低來到 6.6999，而後收斂跌幅，終場境外人民
幣收在 6.6737，週線升值 9 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6714，週線升值 12 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間震盪。就國際市場來看，美國 10 月零售銷售溫和增長，
顯示美國需求仍具韌性，加上聯準會官員偏鷹派談話持續支撐美元，美元指數逼近
104 整數關卡，將限制人民幣升值空間。就中國國內市場來看，近期出口結匯需求
持續提供人民幣支撐，加上 11 月至農曆春節前，企業資金運用及薪資發放等季節性
因素，預計結匯需求仍可望緩解人民幣貶值壓力；而人行中間價維持偏強設定，但
並未持續走升，顯示人行有意維持人民幣穩定，避免升值速度過快。最後就技術面
來看，USD/CNH 小時圖在快速升至 6.7000 附近後回落，顯示上方仍有賣壓待消化，
目前匯價在 20 日及 55 日均線附近整理，短線仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估
本週人民幣區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6600-6.6900

美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/23/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓小幅走升。週初，美國 10 月零售銷售符合市場預期，USD/JPY 大致於
159 上方波動；週間，多位聯準會官員發表偏鷹言論，USD/JPY 最高來到 159.96；
臨近週末，全球股市遭拋售，避險情緒升溫帶動 USD/JPY 回落，終場收在 158.53，
週線下跌 110 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本第三季 GDP 年化意外
萎縮 0.1%，終止連續三季的成長趨勢，主要是生活成本持續攀升，消費支出疲弱拖
累整體經濟活動，不利日圓表現。二、日本央行審議委員高田創表示，考量基礎通
膨已達到 2%，日本央行必須盡早將政策利率上調至適當水準。此外，日本央行總裁
植田和男在經濟委員會上亦表示，近期薪資成長相當強勁，樂見薪資與物價漲幅適
度同步上升，通膨持續達到央行 2%目標，日本央行將適時調整貨幣政策。市場預期
日本央行最快將於 12 月升息，料將支撐日圓走勢。三、聯準會理事沃勒 (Christopher 
Waller)表示通膨範圍持續擴大，認為聯準會應於 12 月政策會議升息 2 碼，抑或釋放
明年加快升息步伐的訊號，聯準會官員偏鷹言論令美元持穩，預計將限制日圓走升
空間。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 各天期均線趨平，匯價多次向上
測試 160 未能有效突破，顯示上方有明顯壓力，下方則有年線支撐。綜合以上，預
估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.20-160.00

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/23/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪走跌。週初，歐元區公布 9 月工業產出低於市場預期，
EUR/USD 跌破 1.10 整數關卡來到 1.0990；週間，多位聯準會官員發表偏鷹言論，
EUR/USD 持續下探至 1.0952；臨近週末，歐元區公布 11 月消費者信心指數高於預
期，EUR/USD 未再進一步走跌，終場收在 1.0967，週線下跌 65 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、德國央行在月度經濟報告
中表示，德國經濟增速可能將於今年第四季回升，經濟增長意外強勁主要受益於穩
健的出口需求和財政刺激措施。市場預估德國今年全年經濟增幅將逾 1%，優於前一
年度的 0.3%，經濟表現強勁預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、聯準會理事巴爾 (Michael Barr)
表示，美國經濟整體表現良好，失業率處於低位，消費者支出穩健，企業投資亦維
持強勁，惟通膨水準仍然過高，若經濟發展與目前的預期一致，巴爾認為進一步升
息是適當的，聯準會官員言論偏鷹，FedWatch 顯示聯準會 12 月升息一碼機率逾八
成，美元持穩，預計將抑制歐元走升空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
各天期均線向下發散，空方動能較強，惟匯價在跌破 1.10 整數關卡後，匯價多次下
探 1.0960 附近暫獲支撐未有效跌破，加上震盪指標 RSI 已進入超賣區，EUR/USD
短線有望觸底回升。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

均未成交，週五等殖系統參考報價中價分別為 1.93%、1.73%。

準會官員近期偏鷹談話強化美國升息預期，並連帶提高台灣央行升息可能性，
致台債買盤追價動力有限，觀望氣氛濃厚，預期台債殖利率維持區間整理，預
估 5 年券區間為 1.70%~1.75%，10 年券區間為 1.90%~1.95%。

Date: 2026-11-12

Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Accton’s 3Q26 results, released on 12 November.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat/miss expectations. Accton’s 3Q26 
net profit came in at TWD13.1bn (EPS: TWD23.3), in line with our estimate 
(TWD13.0bn) but 6% lower than the Bloomberg consensus (TWD13.9bn).  
Revenue was TWD120.7bn (+26% QoQ, 66% YoY), higher than our/BBG 
estimates by 13%/2%, mainly due to increasing AI card contribution for 
Trainium 3. Nonetheless, gross margin fell by 2.1pp QoQ to 17.6% (vs. our 
19.1% estimate), reflecting rising material costs (especially from memory). 
Consequently, OPM declined by 1.6pp to 13.4% (vs. our 15.1% estimate), 
which was slightly offset by better operating leverage.

Business outlook. We believe gross margin is likely the key highlight of 
the 3Q26 results. Per management, gross margin was diluted due to rising 
memory costs. As such, Accton has shifted its business model from “Buy-&-
Sell” to consignment from September. Since then, we see gross margin 
gradually recovering back to the 20% level. Looking into 4Q26, we believe 
Accton could sustain the gross margin, but the networking chipset shortage 
might weigh on revenue momentum in the short term. The alternative is 
that one of the key switch clients could shift its orders slightly towards 
Spectrum first to ease the near-term shortage issue, in our view. As for the 
AI card business, we believe the total shipment has been largely stable 
since 3Q26. Even when the end-client gradually shifts its product mix from 
air-cooled to the liquid-cooled version, we believe the rising content from 
the liquid-cooled version should be able to offset the slightly lower ASP per 
chipset given there are more chipsets (4x vs. 2x from air-cooled) on an 
ASIC AI motherboard. Looking into 2027, we maintain our original view that 
total AMZN ASIC units will grow c.30% YoY to c.3m as the first growth 
drivers. On top of this, the liquid-cooled version has adopted the PCIE 
scale-up switch, which can expand TAM for Accton, in our view. Lastly, for 
the top-of-rack (ToR) switches, we expect 800G switches to remain the 
mainstream models in 2027 but with rising enterprise usage, which could 
likely be the next growth driver in 2027.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 6-12% to factor in 
higher ToR switch revenue. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating, but lower our 
12M TP to TWD3,000 (prior: TWD3,666), based on a target PER multiple of 
24x (prior: 35x, within its past-3-year range of 10-33x), applied to our 1-
year forward EPS forecast, to reflect the weaker market sentiment. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected ASIC server and switch demand.

How we differ: Our 2027E EPS is 5% higher than the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely due to our more positive view on operating leverage.

12 November 2026

3Q26 review: gross margin dilution on memory costs

➢ Fundamentals largely on track with our prior estimates  
➢ Recent pullbacks provide good entry points to build positions 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12M TP to TWD3,000

Company profile 
Founded in 1988, Accton is dedicated to the design and manufacturing of enterprise networking and 
communication products. With its long-term experience and expertise, Accton is one of the leading 
open-source networking ODM partners for major players worldwide, with particular focuses on data 
centres, telco carriers and campus networks. Major products include enterprise switches, metro access 
switches, network applications and wireless products.

### Page 5

5

Accton Technology (2345 TT): 12 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 12 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Accton’s board has 7 directors, with 3 independent directors. The independent directors account
for 43% of all directors. A total of 8 board meetings were held in 2025, and the average actual
attendance rate was 95%.
The backgrounds of independent directors are diversified and their competence includes
business management, accounting, finance, and work experience in the medical industry. We
deem the diversified background as a positive factor for the company’s board quality.
Besides, the Chairman and President are not the same person, which increases the board and
executive management’s quality. Based on its board structure and key executive hiring, we
believe that Accton has already professionalised the family business by allowing external
professionals to run the business for major shareholders.
Capital management 1
Accton declared DPS of TWD15.0 in 2025, representing a 31.8% payout ratio. The ratio was
lower than the 51.2% in 2024 and its historical range of 69-75% from 2018 to 2022. That said, it
still points towards a friendly shareholder dividend policy, in our view.
We attribute the lower payout ratio mainly to the sharp earnings growth and continued capacity
investments in Taiwan and Vietnam amid US-China tensions. The DPS CAGR in the past 10
years was c.23%, which showcased Accton’s capability in capital management. The continuous
improvement in networking capabilities (eg, 400G and 800G switches), alongside expansion into
AI accelerator modules, has helped it differentiate itself from other competitors and strengthen its
position in the white-box switch supply chain for major US cloud service providers.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, revenue from related parties accounted for 0.15% of total revenue, while purchases
from related parties accounted for 2.0% of cost of sales. We see limited risk from related-party
transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
Like most vendors, Accton asks its suppliers to follow: 1) the hazard substance requirements of
the RoHS, Reach and corporate social responsibilities (5 categories, including labour, health and
safety, environmental, ethics and management system) required by the RBA Code of Conduct,
and 2) QC08000 (Hazardous Substances Process Management), while the company conducts
annual reviews to evaluate its suppliers’ performance.
To effectively track, communicate and manage its supply chain to manufacture green products,
each supplier can use the eGreen system® platform, developed independently by Accton, to
learn about environmental protection control and management requirements for components and
materials.
S Data security 2
To ensure information security for customers and the company, Accton obtained ISO/IEC
27001:2013 certification in 2016 and completed the transition to ISO/IEC 27001:2022 in 2024,
with continued verification of its information security policies and controls in 2025. Apart from the
global information security standards, Accton also takes measures to improve its information
security, including:1) Implementing business continuity and disaster recovery mechanisms,
including annual recovery drills for critical systems and databases. In 2025, the company
achieved its target of recovering critical systems within 48 hours. 2) Conducting monthly
vulnerability scans across company-wide servers, network equipment and core systems to
identify potential security risks. Accton also conducts quarterly social engineering drills to
strengthen employee awareness of cybersecurity threats.
In 2025, Accton conducted four phishing simulation exercises involving over 13,000 participant
attendances, achieving an average phishing safe rate of 93%. The company reported no major
information security incidents or personal data breaches during the year.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

高毛利產品合計來到 59%，此外營運槓桿帶來的效益使本業獲利
率創歷史新高。Interplex 已完整併表，單月貢獻金額來到 6700
萬美金，除了擴大產品線外，合併後同樣有助於加深既有客戶的合
作關係。展望 2027 年，HPC 事業將迎來產品價量齊揚，尤其以高
毛利的 Power 產品最為明顯，根據客戶規劃，1.6T AEC 也將逐步
起量，因此可預期 HPC 占比將會再進一步提升，利於整體營運表
現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2026 年 EPS 76.23 元，2027 年
EPS 131.41 元，目前 PE 為 11.6x，參考歷史 PE 區間為 10-35x，
考量貿聯獲利將維持高成長期，唯獨近期市場擔憂資料中心建置速
度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部屬之風險，
因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。

貿聯-KY(3665)成立於 1996 年，為全球連接線大廠，總部位於美
國矽谷，生產據點則廣泛分布在全球 37 地。產品應用包含數據通
訊、工廠自動化、半導體設備、醫療、車用等，2025 年工業佔 35%、
通訊 42%、車用 11%、電器 11%、其他 1%。銷售主要為美國 20%、
中國 20%、德國 16%、馬來西亞 7%、台灣 3%、其他 34%。

殖利率(%) 0.87 HPC 部門成長 Q+30%，Y+61%，SPE 也延續上半年以來的穩健
成長幅度，Q+11%，Y+70%，兩高利潤事業合計貢獻營收占比進

桿效益下也反映在營益率創公司歷史新高。

Interplex 正式併表，產品組合有望再度優化 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.5 億元，M+26.9%，Y+80.0%，自 9 月起完成
對 Interplex 的收購，10 月已完整貢獻營收，單月貢獻金額高達
6700 萬美金，約 20 億台幣，由於 Interplex 既有客戶與貿聯本身
有所重疊，然而產品項目卻有所不同，預計在透過集團整合能力下
將進一步推動公司在客戶群內滲透率的提升。此外貿聯本身新規格
的 Power 產品也持續提高出貨，研究部預期產品組合有望再度優
化，進一步推升獲利率表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 26Q4 營
收 349.1 億元，Q+25.3%，Y+76.5%，毛利率 33.84%，稅後淨利
56.7 億元，Q+32.0%，Y+106.8%，EPS 28.39 元；2026 年營收
1069.3 億元，Y+50.1%，毛利率 31.83%，稅後淨利 152.1 億元，
Y+69.0%，EPS 76.23 元。

2027 年 HPC 產品維持價量齊揚，光通訊產品 2028 年有望發酵 
隨著 Vera Rubin 進入放量，Rack Busbar 升級至 5000A，Power 
Whip 規格從 60A 升級至 100A 且用量增加，1.6T AEC 也將逐步
開始貢獻營收，另外 Interplex 的加入所帶來的產品優化和客戶關
係的綁定，研究部認為貿聯 HPC 事業仍將迎來顯著的價量齊揚增
長。光傳輸領域上貿聯也持續耕耘，除了新富生 MPO 相關產品，
OCP 上與 ams Osram 的合作也意味著公司已著手布局下一代
Scale up 的通訊方案，研究部看好 wide-and-slow 架構在未來的導
入機會以及市場，預計 2028 年後的發酵有望帶來營運成長動能。
綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2027 年營收 1608.0 億元，Y+50.4%，

結論 
高毛利產品合計來到 59%，此外營運槓桿帶來的效益使本業獲利率創歷
史新高。Interplex 已完整併表，單月貢獻金額來到 6700 萬美金，除了
擴大產品線外，合併後同樣有助於加深既有客戶的合作關係。展望 2027
年，HPC 事業將迎來產品價量齊揚，尤其以高毛利的 Power 產品最為明
顯，根據客戶規劃，1.6T AEC 也將逐步起量，因此可預期 HPC 占比將
會再進一步提升，利於整體營運表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2026
年 EPS 76.23 元，2027 年 EPS 131.41 元，目前 PE 為 11.6x，參考歷
史 PE 區間為 10-35x，考量貿聯獲利將維持高成長期，唯獨近期市場擔
憂資料中心建置速度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部
屬之風險，因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요삼성바이오로직스(207940) 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시_dcb09b7c.pdf

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

Company Brief 
제약/바이오 
Analyst 김준영 
junyoung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 김현비 
hyunbee.kim@meritz.co.kr 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요

3Q26 Review  
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(YoY +18.5%, QoQ +12.8%), 영업이익 
6,401억원(YoY +1.0%, QoQ +9.2%)으로 컨센서스를 각각 9.8%, 4.7% 상회했다. 
기존 1 ~ 4공장 풀가동 및 미국 Rockville 공장 매출 약 1,000억원과 5공장 PPQ 
물량 인식이 더해지며 외형 성장을 견인했다. 반면 신규 생산시설의 초기 비용 및 제
품 믹스 영향으로 OPM은 42.9%를 기록하며 전년 동기 대비 7.5%p 하락했다. 동
사는 연간 매출 성장률 가이던스를 기존 +15~20%에서 +20%로 상향했다. 당사는 
4분기 Rockville 공장 매출 기여와 기존 공장의 높은 가동률을 감안하여 연간 매출
액 5조 6,135억원(YoY +23.2%)을 예상한다.

신규 생산거점의 외형 확대, 이익 기여는 좀 더 긴 호흡으로 
5공장은 2Q26부터 PPQ 생산을 본격화했으나 상업 생산에 따른 실질적인 이익 기
여는 2027년 하반기에 확대될 전망이다. Rockville 공장은 3Q와 4Q 각각 약 
1,000억원의 매출이 예상되며 2027년 분기별 매출도 유사한 수준을 유지할 것으로 
전망한다. 다만 영업이익 기여까지는 시간이 더 소요될 것으로 예상되어 2027년 이
익 성장은 미국 공장보다 5공장 Ramp-up이 주도할 전망이다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스에 대해 투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원을 유지한다. 고객사 수주 
문의가 회복되는 가운데 대형 계약 협의가 진행 중이며 6공장 역시 연말 또는 내년 
초 착공을 목표로 하고 있다. 다만 신규 수주가 실제 계약으로 이어지는 시점과 5공
장의 상업 생산 전환 속도가 중기 실적의 핵심 변수로 판단한다. 추가 대형 수주 확
보 시 5공장 이후의 성장 가시성도 높아질 것으로 예상한다. 
PolyPeptide 인수는 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업을 확장한다는 점에서 긍정적이다. 인
수 완료 이후 다음 분기부터 연결 매출 인식이 가능하나 유의미한 영업이익 기여까지
는 최소 2년 이상 소요될 전망이다. PolyPeptide 인수는 현재 추정치에서 반영하지 
않았으며 향후 인수 완료 시 연결 OPM 희석 가능성을 감안할 필요가 있다. 한편 최
근 법원 항고심에서 배양부터 정제까지의 생산공정이 쟁의행위 기간에도 정상 수행되
어야 할 보전작업으로 인정되면서 파업에 따른 생산 차질 우려는 완화될 것으로 판단
한다. 노사 협상은 여전히 진행 중이나 생산 안정성에 대한 고객사 우려가 낮아질 경
우 신규 수주에도 긍정적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 22

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Xuất hiện áp lực điều chỉnh sau khi xuyên thủng kênh hỗ trợ trung hạn, tuy nhiên

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -46.14 điểm tương ứng -2.66%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Công nghệ, Bảo hiểm và Logistics là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,290 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, VCB và ACB; mặt khác, tự doanh duy trì bán ròng ở mức -864 
tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -10,335 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -22,338 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì bán ròng -842 hợp 
đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,945 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua tuần điều chỉnh với 4 phiên giảm điểm liên tiếp, VN-Index đánh 
mất mốc tâm lý 1.700 điểm trong bối cảnh thanh khoản tiếp tục duy trì ở mức thấp và nhà 
đầu tư nước ngoài đẩy mạnh bán ròng, tập trung ở nhóm ngân hàng, Vingroup và FPT. 
Hiệu ứng tích cực từ các động thái hỗ trợ thanh khoản của NHNN qua nghiệp vụ mua vào 
ngoại tệ nhanh chóng hạ nhiệt, khi các phiên chào mua của NHNN đều diễn ra không 
thành công do tỷ giá bật tăng trở lại. Bên ngoài, TTCK toàn cầu tiếp tục chịu áp lực khi 
nhóm cổ phiếu AI biến động mạnh và kết quả kinh doanh tích cực của Nvidia không đủ 
xoa dịu lo ngại về đầu tư vào AI, cùng với kỳ vọng Fed nâng lãi suất trong tháng 12, gây 
thêm sức ép lên dòng vốn vào các thị trường mới nổi. Điểm tích cực là lực cầu bắt đáy 
xuất hiện trong phiên cuối tuần giúp chỉ số thu hẹp đà giảm và đóng cửa ở mức cao nhất 
phiên.

TCT Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam (ACV) 
Áp lực lợi nhuận trong chu kỳ mở rộng công suất 
19/11/2026

KQKD 3Q2026 suy giảm do khấu hao 
và biến động tỷ giá

Lượt khách ACV dự kiến tăng chậm 
hơn toàn ngành do tác động từ Phú 
Quốc và Gia Bình

Long Thành dự kiến khai thác từ cuối 
2026, tạo áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận 
giai đoạn đầu

Duy trì khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá 
mục tiêu 39,500 VND/cổ phiếu

3Q2026, ACV ghi nhận doanh thu đạt 6,066 tỷ VND (-6% yoy) và LNST công ty mẹ 
đạt 2,206 tỷ VND (-14% yoy). Lợi nhuận suy giảm chủ yếu do (1) khấu hao tăng từ 
các nhà ga mới và (2) ghi nhận lỗ tỷ giá 168 tỷ VND trong kỳ, trái ngược với khoản lãi 
tỷ giá cùng kỳ 2025. Lũy kế 9M2026, doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ lần lượt đạt 
19,226/7,435 tỷ VND (+0%/+2% yoy).

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế/nội địa qua hệ thống ACV tăng trưởng bình 
quân 7%/5.5%/năm đến 2030, thấp hơn toàn ngành do (1) sân bay Phú Quốc 
không còn thuộc hệ thống ACV từ đầu 2026 và (2) một phần khách quốc tế tại Nội 
Bài dịch chuyển tự nhiên sang sân bay Gia Bình khi đi vào khai thác. Mức độ dịch 
chuyển trước năm 2030 dự kiến còn hạn chế do phụ thuộc vào tiến độ hoàn thiện 
sân bay và hạ tầng giao thông kết nối.

Cảng HKQT Long Thành giai đoạn 1 dự kiến khai thác một phần từ cuối tháng 12/2026 
và bắt đầu tiếp nhận các chuyến bay quốc tế đường dài từ Tân Sơn Nhất vào ngày 
28/03/2027, bổ sung công suất 25 triệu khách/năm cho ACV. Tuy nhiên, tốc độ 
chuyển dịch có thể chậm hơn kế hoạch do hạ tầng kết nối chưa hoàn thiện, trong khi 
chi phí khấu hao và vận hành tăng dự kiến gây áp lực lên biên lợi nhuận của ACV giai 
đoạn 2027–2028.

Sử dụng phương pháp chiết khấu dòng tiền, chúng tôi duy trì khuyến nghị TRUNG 
LẬP đối với ACV với giá mục tiêu 39,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng 
giá 4.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 18/11/2026.

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành nến giảm điểm thân dài, thể hiện áp lực phân phối đang có 
phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, sau lần thứ 3 nỗ lực vượt cản 1.750 bất thành, chỉ số đã quay 
đầu lao dốc và xuyên thủng kênh hỗ trợ trung hạn, đồng thời phá vỡ cạnh dưới của một 
mẫu hình tam giác nhỏ, kết thúc nhịp hồi phục tích lũy ngắn hạn. Mặc dù những tín hiệu 
này đều mang tính tiêu cực, tuy nhiên khi đặt trong bức tranh rộng hơn, những chuyển 
biến này chưa gây ra rủi ro quá lớn đến xu hướng đi ngang chủ đạo và chúng tôi kỳ vọng 
VNINDEX sẽ xuất hiện các nhịp hồi phục trở lại, ngay sau khi chớm xuyên thủng các vùng 
đáy ngắn/trung hạn. Yếu tố cần chú ý giai đoạn này là sự phân hóa rõ nét với cơ hội tìm 
kiếm lợi nhuận chỉ tập trung ở nhóm có đủ 2 yếu tố: nền tảng cơ bản tốt và đã về lại các 
vùng hỗ trợ mạnh trên khung lớn.  
(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports.1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.
Exhibit 1: FDI Inflows to the U.S.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.

areas such as defense, data center

On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising

from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026,2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

the U.S. over developed economies in

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE: DOUBLE-DIGIT GROWTH IN BUSINESS CREATIONS,
LOWER INCREASE IN BANKRUPTCIES, AND STABLE NON-PERFORMING LOANS – PARTIAL UPDATE WITH
MONTHLY DATA AS OF SEPTEMBER 2026
ECO CHARTS | BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IS A NEW PUBLICATION AIMED AT PROVIDING AN OVERVIEW OF THE DYNAMICS
OF BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE, AS WELL AS THERISKS ASSOCIATED WITH THE LATTER. WE WILL PUBLISH

REGULAR UPDATES TO THIS PUBLICATION.

### Page 2

Double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans
2ECOCHARTSECONOMIC STUDIES
• Business creations rose by 16% y/y as of Q3 2026, marking an acceleration compared with the growth seen in 2025 (5.3% y/y).
• This increase was driven by sectors associated with new information and communication technologies, where the trend is gaining
momentum.
• Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 1.7% y/y as of Q3 2026, according to the data from the Banque de France (vs. 3.6% y/y in

2025). After a more pronounced rise in the first quarter (8% y/y), this increase has since slowed. This moderation could continue, as,
according to Altares, payment delays have decreased to an average of 12.3 days in the second quarter of 2026 (14.1 days a year
earlier), compared with the European average of 13.1 days.
• The rise in business bankruptcies is most pronounced in the services sector, which has simultaneously seen an increase in business

creations. This trend is clear in the retail sector, with a 26% y/y increase in business creations as of Q3 2026, while business
bankruptcies are up 3% y/y.
• In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been declining since Q2 2026.
• The level of the average ratio of non-performing loans is moderately high and has remained stable for the past year (4.0% of total
loans and advances in Q2 2026). More cyclical than in other sectors, the ratio of non-performing loans in construction reached its

highest level since 2019, at 9.5%.
• The share of underperforming loans, but not yet non-performing (in Stage 2, according to IFRS 9 terminology), which provides an
indication of the future trend in non-performing loans, is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all-time high (16.1%).
• Despite a significant rise since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies — which allows them to be weighted by a proxy for
company size — remains in September, on average, below its Q1 2025 peak (0.54 compared with 0.61, respectively). The economic

weight of bankrupted small businesses decreased but still stands at a high level (2.03).
For more information, please refer to our publication: Chart of the week | French corporates: peaks in creations put peaks in bankruptcies into
perspective. July 22, 2026 – Economic Research – BNP Paribas

Survey of business creations (all types, companies, sole traders, micro-entrepreneurs). Monthly data from

Gross non performing loans and advances at amortised cost / Total gross loans and advances at
amortised cost

Loans and advances in Stage 2 / Total loans and advances. Loans and advances at amortised cost and at
fair value through equity
Economic weight of bankruptcies Banque de France Monthly
Total outstanding bank loans to newly bankrupt NFCs over a one-year period, calculated as the figure for

the month in which the firm is declared bankrupt, relative to the average outstanding bank loans to both
healthy and bankrupt NFCs during the same period.

### Page 1

After resilient growth this year, several headwinds will likely weigh on the n
economy in 2027. We expect high energy prices to slow consumption over the 
winter, tighter credit conditions to curb demand and France to stagnate until the 
elections. But global manufacturing (supported by AI capex), German fiscal 
expansion and continued structural expansion in Southern Europe should 
cushion these headwinds. We expect Euro area growth of 0.9% in 2027, below 
the recent run rate, estimated potential and consensus.  
High energy prices will likely keep headline inflation near 4% through the winter, n
while indirect effects push core inflation to a 2.9% peak in Q2. But we expect a 
short-lived hump given limited wage second-round effects, with sequential 
inflation pressures largely normalising in 2027H2. 
Above-target inflation and recent ECB commentary point to one more rate hike n
in December. A March move is possible with higher energy prices and stronger 
data, but the hurdle to faster hikes is high. We expect normalisation cuts from 
2027Q4 as the inflation shock fades, leaving our rate path below market pricing. 
Intensifying French budget tensions are the main risk. Polls suggest that Marine n
Le Pen is most likely to become President. If she is elected and wins a majority in 
Parliament, investors could question her commitment to reduce the deficit. If the 
elections fail to deliver a parliamentary majority, the prospects of continued high 
deficits could further weigh on domestic assets. French fiscal tensions could spill 
over into other high-debt countries, but the risk of area-wide contagion looks 
notably lower than in 2011-15. 
Europe also faces structural headwinds from lost competitiveness, rising n
competition from China and demographic changes. Growth industries (such as 
defence), widespread AI adoption and further internal market integration) offer 
significant opportunities, although the upcoming elections will likely hinder 
major reform progress in the near term.

### Page 2

Euro Area 2027 Outlook: Resilience Put to the Test

Following surprisingly resilient Euro area growth this year, we expect 2027 to be more 
challenging. We forecast area-wide growth of 0.9% next year, slightly below the recent 
run rate, our estimate of potential and consensus (Exhibit 1). But we expect growth to 
improve in H2 as headwinds fade and see significant variation across the Euro area, with 
firm growth in Germany and Spain, subdued momentum in Italy and stagnation in 
France.

Rising Headwinds 
We expect several headwinds to slow growth next year, especially in H1. 
First, high energy prices are likely to weigh on consumption over the winter (Exhibit 2). 
Our commodity strategists expect Brent oil and European TTF gas prices to remain 
elevated given continued disruption of Middle East supplies, with risks towards renewed 
escalation and higher prices. As a result, high headline inflation is likely to weigh 
materially on real disposable income over the winter and slow consumer spending. 
Consistent with this, consumer confidence has dropped since the summer and retail 
sales have weakened, especially in Germany.

Exhibit 1: : We Expect Trend-Like Growth in 2027

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg
13 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 3

Second, tighter credit conditions look set to slow growth (Exhibit 3). Following recent 
easing, our area-wide financial condition index (GSFCI) is now only modestly tighter 
since the summer, as a weaker trade-weighted Euro has mitigated the effects of higher 
long-term yields and wider credit spreads. But survey-based measures of bank lending 
have deteriorated notably, especially in France. As a result, credit conditions are likely to 
act as a moderate headwind to growth next year, particularly on rate-sensitive domestic 
activity (such as housing and business investment).

Third, we expect the French economy to stagnate until the elections (Exhibit 4). In 
addition to tightening financial conditions, heightened political and policy uncertainty 
are likely to lead households and firms to postpone major consumption and investment 
decisions. Fiscal policy is likely to remain a contractionary force, because a large 
increase in interest payments will likely mask a modest consolidation in the primary 
balance. We forecast annual average growth of 0.1% in 2027, well below other

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg

Exhibit 3: Tighter Credit Conditions Will Weigh on Growth

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics
13 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 4

forecasters and the weakest forecast among the developed economies we cover.

Sources of Resilience 
That said, we see several sources of resilience, which should buffer these headwinds and 
help growth recover in H2. 
First, we expect a continued boost from the global manufacturing cycle (Exhibit 5). The 
international industrial upswing continues, boosting factory orders and exports, 
especially in Germany. AI capex is an important driver behind this pickup, likely lifting 
Euro area growth by 0.1pp this year and next. Given spare capacity in European 
manufacturing—particularly in Germany—we see a continued cyclical export tailwind 
ahead.

Second, fiscal policy will continue to support growth in Germany (Exhibit 6). We remain 
constructive on the German fiscal rollout driven by higher defence spending and

Exhibit 4: We Expect Stagnation in France as Fiscal Uncertainty Builds

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics

Exhibit 5: A Continued Boost from Global Manufacturing, Including AI Capex

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics
13 November 2026   4
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 5

improved execution of infrastructure investment. We expect the German deficit to rise 
to 4.6% next year (from 4% in 2026) and boost German growth by 0.6pp (vs 0.7pp this 
year). That said, fiscal policy looks set to turn contractionary in France and Spain as 
national consolidation pressures build and the Recovery Fund expires. As a result, we see 
a neutral area-wide fiscal stance in 2027 after a small net boost this year, before turning 
contractionary in 2028.

Third, we see continued structural expansion in Southern Europe (Exhibit 7). Spain, in 
particular, has continued to outgrow the Euro area materially, driven by strong gains in 
employment (underpinned by immigration), higher-value-added services (including 
finance and ICT) and investment. While momentum is likely to moderate next year, we 
see continued outperformance in the South.

### Page 6

Modest Underlying Inflation Pressures 
High energy prices (including product spreads) are likely to keep headline inflation close 
to 4% through the winter (Exhibit 8). Headline inflation could climb even higher if upside 
risks to our commodity price forecasts materialise, with headline inflation peaking close 
to 6% in an adverse scenario for oil and gas. We also see building pressures on food 
inflation next year, reflecting passthrough effects from higher energy prices, the ongoing 
Russia/Ukraine war and global weather patterns.

We do not, however, look for material passthrough effects from headline to underlying 
inflation (Exhibit 9). So far, we see little evidence of indirect effects of higher energy 
prices into core inflation, with muted movement in energy-intensive core goods prices. 
Instead, we estimate that most of the recent pickup in core goods inflation reflects 
AI-related equipment. Given pipeline pressures in import and producer prices as well as 
airfares, we expect more notable indirect effects to build in coming months, pushing 
core inflation up.

Exhibit 8: A Sharp Pickup in Headline Inflation

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics
13 November 2026   6
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 7

But we expect the core inflation hump to be short-lived given signs of limited 
second-round effects (Exhibit 10). Measures of wage growth have remained contained 
despite the surge in energy prices, including forward-looking surveys (such as the Indeed 
and ECB wage trackers) and recent wage agreements. While we have slightly raised our 
forecast for nominal wage growth next year, we expect pay gains to return to their 
normal range in 2028, driven by moderating labour demand.

Taken together, we expect core inflation to peak at 2.9% in Q2, significantly above the 
ECB’s target and somewhat above the recent staff projections (Exhibit 11), partly 
reflecting firmer energy price assumptions. But we expect the overshoot to be notably 
more limited and temporary than in 2022-23, with sequential inflation pressures largely 
normalising in 2027H2.

Exhibit 9: Modest Passthrough Effects Ahead

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research

Exhibit 10: Little Risk of Second-Round Effects

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, ECB
13 November 2026   7
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 8

Limited ECB Tightening 
Above-target inflation, ongoing growth and signs that the neutral rate might have edged 
up point to additional ECB tightening, consistent with recent ECB communication and 
our macro model simulations (Exhibit 12). We therefore view one more hike at the 
December meeting as likely. Acute sovereign stress could still lead the Council to hold 
rates in December, but the required tightening in financial conditions would likely need 
to be large given the ongoing energy pressures.

An additional hike in March is possible if energy prices continue to rise, core inflation 
firms more than expected, or clearer signs of second-round effects emerge. But we see a 
significant hurdle to speeding up the pace of hikes and raising the deposit rate 
meaningfully above 3%. We expect the Governing Council to start normalising the policy 
rate as the inflation shock fades and sequential core inflation normalises, with cuts in

Exhibit 11: A Limited Inflation Overshoot

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics

Exhibit 12: A December Hike is Very Likely

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics
13 November 2026   8
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 9

December 2027 and March 2028. As a result, our policy rate path remains below market 
pricing, including on a probability-weighted basis.

Risks from a French Budget Crisis 
An intensification of French budget tensions is the main downside risk to our outlook. 
French bonds could already come under stress before the elections if presidential 
candidates do not express sufficient concern regarding the fiscal outlook. Markets would 
probably react negatively to far-left leader Mélenchon progressing to the second round 
of the presidential elections, even though polls suggest he would then be very likely to 
lose against far-right leader Marine Le Pen. Investors could also question Le Pen’s 
commitment to reducing the deficit if she becomes President—as polls currently suggest 
she will—and wins a parliamentary majority. In that respect, we noted that her recent 
economic proposals would likely face significant implementation challenges, as they are 
based on optimistic growth assumptions and include costly measures. Finally, if no 
parliamentary majority emerges after the next elections—to which our model attributes 
a 20% probability— bond markets would likely react negatively to the prospect of 
continued high deficits, political uncertainty and low growth. As a result, we expect 
French government bonds to remain under pressure.

Exhibit 13: Our ECB Views Remain Dovish Compared with Market Pricing

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, ECB
13 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 10

An escalation of French budget tensions could spill over into other high-debt bond 
markets (Exhibit 15). Fiscal imbalances are elevated, interest rates are high, and debt 
dynamics look concerning in several countries (including Italy, Belgium, Austria and 
Finland). Financial-market vulnerabilities also persist through sovereign exposures and 
non-bank leverage. Upcoming elections in Italy may complicate the fiscal adjustment 
required, while Spain’s fiscal outlook remains encouraging (despite the November 29 
election). Also, failure to agree on the 2028-35 EU Budget could bring into question 
fiscal cohesion.

That said, the Euro area today is stronger in several important respects than during the 
European sovereign debt crisis (Exhibit 16). Nominal growth is materially higher, 
household finances are more robust, external imbalances are smaller, and banks—
especially in Southern Europe—are much healthier. The Euro area has also strengthened 
its institutional architecture through the ESM and greater common EU issuance.

Exhibit 14: : The Risk of a French Budget Crisis

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Polymarket

Exhibit 15: European Sovereign Risk is Back in Focus

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, IMF
13 November 2026   10
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 11

Moreover, the ECB could activate its anti-fragmentation tools in the event of extreme 
bond market pressures (Exhibit 17), including the Transmission Protection Instrument 
(TPI). But our analysis suggests that ECB intervention would likely require significantly 
more intense and broad-based sovereign stress, especially with inflation well above 
target. In particular, our new index—constructed to predict ECB bond market 
interventions—suggests that fragmentation risk remains limited despite the recent 
widening in spreads.

Structural Challenges and Opportunities  
Europe continues to struggle with several structural headwinds. The Euro area has lost 
international competitiveness and faces rising competition with China, which is likely to 
act as a steady headwind and push the EU towards a more assertive trade stance. Also, 
Europe has underinvested in growth sectors, offers a less attractive business

Exhibit 16: The Euro Area is Stronger than in 2011-15

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Polymarket
13 November 2026   11
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 12

environment than its DM peers and lacks cross-country harmonisation in regulation. 
Given a significant demographic headwind, we see area-wide potential growth at only 
1%, and declining later in the decade.

That said, we also see significant growth opportunities through reforms, as outlined in 
the Draghi report. In particular, Europe could improve its economic performance 
through well-targeted public investment (including defence), leadership in growth 
industries (such as pharmaceuticals and green technologies) and widespread adoption 
of AI. Estimates also point to significant growth upside from financial market reform 
(building on Germany’s pension reform) and further integration of the internal market 
(including EU Inc to support firm creation).

While the upcoming national elections will likely complicate major progress in coming 
months, it remains essential that policymakers push reforms that lead to a lasting 
improvement in Europe’s economic performance. We will, in particular, monitor efforts

### Page 1

2026 was the year of strong earnings. Global equity returns were unusually n
broad-based across regions, supported primarily by robust earnings growth — 
valuations have moderated. 
Earnings growth is poised to slow. As we enter 2027, earnings growth is on n
course to slow sharply across all major regions, growth in AI capex spending is 
likely to peak and the cost of capital remains elevated ... 
... But equities have de-rated sharply; with more attractive valuations, n
positive but slower nominal economic and earnings growth, and lower bond 
yields, inflation and oil prices, we expect equities to continue to rise, but 
with high volatility. 
We focus on Growth balanced with selective Defensives and beneficiaries of n
lower bond yields. 
Attractive opportunities are emerging in long-duration Growth companies, n
which should become more valuable as growth rates slow, particularly US 
Hyperscalers, which continue to enjoy strong (although slower) earnings growth 
and balance sheets. 
We balance this with selective defensive areas that offer reasonable valuations n
and areas that benefit from potential lower bond yields and oil prices. 
While forecast returns are highest in Asia, we focus on risk-adjusted returns and n
are overweight the US and Japan, neutral in Asia and EM, and underweight 
Europe, reflecting relative growth, profitability and valuation opportunities. 
Risks are becoming more two-sided. Bonds, oil prices and AI capex shifts could n
all increase volatility. We recommend diversification across Growth and 
selective Defensive strategies.

### Page 2

Value in Defensive Growth as earnings slow

Our theme for 2026 focused on a broadening equity bull market, with greater 
participation across regions, sectors and themes. Since then, the equity bull market has 
continued, and has widened out across the major regions despite rising oil prices and 
bond yields. The main driver has been strong corporate earnings growth across all 
regions. As we look to 2027, profit and AI capex growth look poised to slow in most 
regions. Nevertheless, recent price action has reflected this slowdown and equity 
valuations have come down substantially, particularly in the Technology sector. 
With economic activity and earnings on course to grow in 2027, albeit at a slower pace 
in nominal terms, and given the prospect of moderating inflation, bond yields and oil 
prices, we expect the equity bull market to continue. The range of potential outcomes 
has widened and, given stock concentration in the US and Asia, is particularly 
dependent on AI capex progress. Our recommended weightings reflect our 
assessment of risk-adjusted returns. We focus on: 
Growth — as earnings growth rates slow and margins moderate, investors are n
likely to reward these companies more highly, particularly as valuations become 
more attractive. AI-related companies have de-rated sharply, reflecting anxiety 
about peak AI capex spend and the possibility of slower returns. This is leaving 
attractive entry points, particularly in large capex Hyperscalers, which have strong 
balance sheets and diversified income streams. Significant upside potential has also 
opened up in more cyclical growth areas in memory and chips, although this comes 
with much higher risks and likely volatility.  
On a risk-adjusted basis, we are overweight the US and Japan, neutral Asia and n
EM, and underweight Europe.  
2026 — the year of earnings 
Key developments in 2026 form the foundations for a shift in opportunities in 2027. 
We see three standout features of global equity markets in 2026. 
Diversification has worked. Equity returns have been reasonable and very 1.
similar across all regions — reversing the trend between 2009 and 2025 when 
equity returns were consistently dominated by US outperformance. 
Strong earnings growth has been the principal driver of equity returns. 2.
All equity markets have de-rated, experiencing lower valuations. 3.
1. Diversified returns 
Overall, the past 12 months have seen broad-based returns across the regions. As 
Exhibit 1 shows, most equity markets have generated very similar total returns (the 
orange diamonds). This reverses the trend that had dominated post financial crisis 
through to 2025, when the US consistently outperformed other regions, benefiting from 
a combination of strong earnings growth and re-rating (as most of the businesses at the 
time were capital-light and high ROE). 
12 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Equity Strategy

### Page 3

2. Earnings have been the main driver of returns 
Earnings revisions have also been strong across the board (Exhibit 2). There are broadly 
three key drivers of this strength. First, Technology earnings have continued to grow 
robustly; second, higher oil prices have boosted earnings across the commodity 
sector; and third, the spill-over effects of the Technology capex boom have 
generally boosted earnings in HALO (heavy asset, low obsolescence) industries, 
such as Industrials. These factors have also contributed to an unusual pattern of 
earnings upgrades for 2026, 2027. So far, despite the recent equity sell-off, earnings 
revisions have also increased for 2028.

### Page 4

While this has been true in every region, the strongest upgrades have come in EM, largely 
reflecting huge upgrades in the Semiconductor space. This matters, because outsized 
gains in Tech Hardware stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — 
Korea and Taiwan now account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% 
and the top 3 stocks (the ‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. 
The recent sell-off in chip names, reflecting fears of a slower roll-out of data centres, has 
pushed valuations down sharply, creating good upside convexity — although the range 
of outcomes is wide and highly dependent on the pace of AI capex growth. See from our 
Equity analysts: At the center of tensions around the AI datacenter build: Searching for value 
following the sector-wide pullback. 
3. Equity markets have de-rated 
Equity valuations have fallen across the board. While the US equity market remains the 
most expensive, it has moderated from close to its 100th percentile over 20 years to a 
P/E of 18.5x.  Asian markets and EM are now trading at P/E ratios well below their 
historical averages. Overall, P/E ratios are around their average over the past 35 years, 
and a similar pattern is true of PEG ratios (adjusting valuations for expected growth) 
(Exhibit 3). Of course, much of the fall in valuations, particularly in the 
Tech-concentrated US and North Asia equity markets, has been the principal driver of 
this de-rating.

### Page 5

The key drivers for 2027 
Equity markets typically rise so long as nominal GDP is growing, valuations are not 
excessive, and interest rates are not rising sharply.  Nevertheless, the momentum of 
growth matters. The shift lower in equity markets in recent weeks — centred around AI 
concerns — has rapidly priced in the prospect of weaker growth rates, and the sharp 
sector and factor rotations within equities have pushed up the valuation of Defensive 
and Value parts of the market. From here, we expect equities to generate positive 
returns (Exhibit 4), albeit with higher volatility than in recent years. We expect investors 
to seek companies that can sustain growth as it becomes more scarce.

Theme 1: Earnings growth is on course to slow 
Our economists expect a slowing in nominal GDP growth rates in 2027 as the AI 
investment boom peaks. At the same time, nearly all markets are likely to see their 
earnings growth rates halve at least (Exhibit 5): in the US, slowing from 36% to 11%, 
Europe from just around 18% to 7%, Japan from 24% to 9% and Asia from close to

Why should earnings growth slow down? 
(a) Hyperscaler capex growth should slow, which should result in decelerating 
earnings growth (Exhibit 6) for much of the AI infrastructure complex and weaken the 
support for some of the ‘picks and shovels’ of the AI-related capex boom, including 
Industrials. Consensus estimates show a slowdown in IT earnings growth from close to 
90% in 2026 to 45% in 2027 and expect Communication Services to slow from growth of 
34% in 2026 to -5% in 2027. (b) Furthermore, depreciation charges should rise 
alongside other costs, such as interest charges. Together, headwinds to growth should 
boost the attractiveness of companies that can sustain higher growth and stable 
margins. This would be relatively good for the US Hyperscalers (whose valuations have 
fallen), but raises more risks for the chip names and Industrial Cyclicals, which have 
enjoyed the benefit of accelerating AI-related capex.

### Page 8

Theme 2: Cost of capital is higher 
Why is the cost of capital higher? 
First, higher inflation has pushed up policy rates. Our rates strategists expect to see a 1.
modest tightening cycle, but slow progress towards cutting interest rates in 2027. 
Second, we have argued that this has reflected the combined impact of a surge in 2.
private sector demand for capital and rising government borrowing. Our bond 
strategists do see some relief in longer-term rates, which should be more supportive 
for equities (Exhibit 8) but our credit strategists expect credit spreads to widen from 
current levels.

Investment Themes 
Broadly, we would focus on three areas within equities: Growth, selective Defensives and 
beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond yields. The expressions that best fit these 
themes vary somewhat across regions and there is some overlap, particularly between 
defensive areas and bond beneficiaries. Exhibit 9 shows a high level summary. More 
details can be found in the specific regional outlook reports.

### Page 9

Growth 
1) Growth versus Value 
For most of the period after the financial crisis through to the end of Covid-19, equity 
markets were dominated by Growth companies. A combination of surging technology 
profitability and ultra-low interest rates favoured Growth. At the same time, 
disinflationary pressures coupled with low energy prices and poor Banks returns 
(particularly in Europe), became headwinds for Value. Since 2022, however, the 
patterns have changed. Growth has still done well, but rising inflation and interest rates, 
coupled with higher energy prices and a capex boom, have strengthened the 
performance of many Value sectors. In all regions Value has sharply outperformed 
Growth in recent months (Exhibit 10).

Europe offers a region where lower oil prices would provide a particularly strong support

Investment focus: Selective Defensives  
The economic outlook remains resilient, but risks are skewed to the downside. Slower 
EPS growth, tighter financial conditions, elevated borrowing costs and the risk of an 
earlier turn in the AI cycle argue for greater portfolio resilience. Bond yields should 
decline modestly, but credit spreads could widen as growth slows. However, Cyclicals 
have already underperformed, while many traditional Defensives no longer look cheap

(Exhibit 16).

We favour selective Defensives rather than a broad defensive shift.  We focus on 
defensive sectors that would also benefit from lower bond yields. Utilities and Telecoms 
in Europe, Healthcare and companies consistently buying back stock in the US, and Asian 
power companies are examples. Quality should benefit from slower growth and tighter 
financial conditions, but valuation discipline remains important. High dispersion should 
create a better environment for stock-picking, with a focus on stable earnings, structural 
growth, strong balance sheets and shareholder returns. Areas of focus include: US—
Healthcare, Power and Buyback Aristocrats; Asia—Shareholder-return strategies, 
secure dividends, strong balance sheets and companies with high and stable 
margins. In Europe, we would include Telecoms and Utilities. 
Investment focus: Selective beneficiaries of lower oil prices and bond 
yields 
Some normalisation in the Middle East and a recovery in Persian Gulf supply should 
reduce geopolitical risk premia and bring down commodity prices, including European 
gas prices. Our commodities team forecast that Brent will fall from around $100/bbl at 
end-2026 to $78/bbl at end-2027, turning the energy-price impulse from a 0.4pp drag 
on global growth into a modest boost in 2027. Combined with fading US tariff effects 
and easing AI-related supply pressures, this should lower inflation and help central 
banks bring it back towards target. The Fed and ECB should move towards rate cuts in 
late 2027, supporting modestly lower bond yields. Our rates forecasts remain more 
dovish than market pricing.  
Investment implications. Lower inflation and bond yields should support long-duration 
Growth and rate-sensitive sectors. Falling energy costs should strengthen consumers’

real incomes across regions and support corporate margins outside the Energy sector. 
Europe should be the clearest beneficiary because of its greater exposure to

exposure, including Consumer Discretionary and long-duration Growth. In Asia, we 
would target selective long-duration and rate-sensitive beneficiaries. In Europe, we 
favour Telecoms, Utilities, UK Real Estate, the DAX and Retailers. 
Overweight the US  
While the US equity market outperformed consistently over the 15 years post the 
financial crisis, its relative performance has faded since 2025. Over the course of 2026, 
the US equity market has seen more of a valuation squeeze than other markets; its ROE 
has increased relative to the rest of the world, while its relative P/B has contracted 
(Exhibit 17). Overall, we see US equity valuations as attractive versus history and also 
compared with other markets when adjusted for growth and likely volatility. We are 
underweight Europe; while lower oil prices would provide an important boost for 
European economies, it would also have a negative impact on earnings growth. 
Valuations have not fallen as much as in other regions, and the risks are higher given 
government debt issues and the heavy political calendar.

The devaluation of emerging market equities has been particularly notable through 
2026 (Exhibit 18). Driven by the AI investment boom, outsized gains in Tech Hardware 
stocks have pushed EM index concentration to record highs — Korea and Taiwan now 
account for about half of the MSCI EM, with Info Tech at >40% and the top 3 stocks (the 
‘AI trio’ of TSMC, Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix) at ~30%. Although the recent 
sell-off leaves room for a potential strong recovery, the risks remain higher than for the 
Hyperscalers. For MXAPJ (incl. Korea/Taiwan), we forecast a 17% USD price return and 
20% total return, supported by 26%/13% EPS growth in 2027/28 and a 9.3x target P/E, 
but Ex-Korea/Taiwan, we forecast 12% USD price return and 15% total return, and

across MXAPJ markets, the median USD total return is 13%, with an average of 15%. 
Given YTD annualised volatility of 24.2% for MXAPJ we recommend a neutral overall 
position for Asia x Japan and EM on a volatility-adjusted basis.

What are the risks? 
We see four in particular: bond volatility, oil volatility, weaker AI capex, and momentum 
concentration — each of which could generate both downside as well as upside risks for 
investors.  
1. The bond market 
The rise in bond yields through 2026 has, so far, largely been offset in equity markets by 
strong earnings growth. That said, it has contributed to lower P/E ratios, and the 
underperformance of sectors that are most sensitive to interest rates, such as Real 
Estate and Defensives, while contributing to the relative strength (until recently) of 
Cyclicals.   
Any relief in bond markets, leading to lower yields, would hamper the relative 
performance of Cyclicals and Financials and favour some of the more defensive 
areas of the equity markets — another reason to have some exposure to selective 
defensives and also sectors that are highly negatively correlated with bond yields.  
Sovereign debt concerns could result in a further spike in bond yields, with the risks 
highest in Europe during an election-heavy year, while an alleviation of inflationary 
pressures might lead to a rally.  
2. Oil prices  
The combination of rising oil prices and bond yields has been central to the movement in 
equity markets through 2026. Our commodity analysts forecast oil at $88/bbl for Brent

### Page 17

and $78/bbl for WTI in 2027, with risks in both directions. In general, lower energy
prices would be good for equity markets, bringing down inflation and interest rate
expectations. On balance, we think this would boost longer duration growth valuations
(from the relatively depressed levels currently), while also supporting defensive growth.
By contrast, such an outcome would reduce profit growth expectations for Energy
companies — a factor that would likely weigh more on Europe given that Energy is a

relatively important contributor to earnings growth.
3. AI capex spend
AI capex spending has been an important support for equity markets over the past three
years, driving earnings growth across the Technology ecosystem, and beyond into the
capex beneficiaries. Our economists expect the AI capex boom to slow. This could lead
to a de-rating in sectors that have become the beneficiaries of the capex boom,
including parts of the Industrials sector, which has re-rated, but also memory and chips

stocks. The latter have de-rated sharply, making the entry point more attractive, but
volatility is likely to remain high.
4. Narrow Breadth & Concentration
Related to this point, the US and Asia equity markets have a high degree of stock
concentration in their indices. Narrow breadth can create more stock volatility, with the
dominant stocks rising or the rest of the market falling. Either way, the momentum factor
risks high volatility and having some diversification across Growth and some Defensives

should help to improve risk-adjusted returns.  From a geographic perspective, the two
most concentrated regions are the US and Asia. That said, given recent market action,
we think the large cap technology names in the US are more defensive that those in Asia.
On a risk-adjusted basis, therefore, we think the momentum and concentration risks are
higher in Asia and imply lower risk-adjusted returns.

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
